This Report has been prepared far submission to the Governor
under At ticle l5l o1 the Constitution.
Chapters I and II of this Report contain Audit observations on
matters arising from examination of Finance Accounls and
Appropriation Accounts of the State Gaverntnent respec'livelyfor the
year ended 3I lularch 2008.
The remaining chapters deal with the findings of perJbrmance audit
and audit af transactions in various deportments including the
Public Works and lwigation Departments, audit of Stores and
Stock, Reventte Receipts, audit of Autonomous Bodies, Statutory
C orp or atio ns. and Goy er nment C ontp ani e s.
The cases mentioned in the Report are those which came to notice in
the course of test audit of accounts during the year 2007-08, as well
as those which had come to notice in earlier years but could not be
deah wfth in the previous Reports; matters relating to the period
subsequent to 2007-08 have also been includedwherever necessary.

SVERVIEW I

This Report includes two chapters containing observations of Audit on the
Finance Accounts and Appropriation Accounts of the State for the year 2007-
08 anri five other chapters with three perfolmance reviews, including
integrated audit of In'igation and Flood Control Department, 23 {excluding
general paragraphs) paragraphs dealing with the results of audit of seiected
schemes, programmes, financial transactions of the Government and its
commerciai and trading activities.
Copies of the perfomrance revie.ws and paragraphs were sent to the
Commissioners/Secretaries of the Deparlments concemed by tlie Accountant
Generai ior fumishing repiies within six weeks. A11 the three reviews and
eieven paragraphs were discussed with the concemed Principal Secretar:ies/
Commissicners/Secretaries and other officers of the State Govemment. In
respect of tweive audit paragraphs, replies had not been received from the
State Government.

The fiscai positiol of the State vier,r,ed in tenns of the key fiscal parameters -
revenue surpius, fiscal deflcit and primary deficit - indicated significant
improvement ciurir-rg 2007-08 over the previous year. Whilo rovoru, surplus
nearly tripled, both frscal and primary deficits tumed into surpius during the
current year. The targets set by FRBI4 Act as w'ell as by TFC/FCP/MTFPS in
terms of deficit indicators were achieved earlier than the time trimit set for
them. The irnprovelxent in fiscal pcsition of the State shor:ld hou.ever be
consiclered keeping ir vierv the fact that signiflcant share (exceeding 90 per
cent) af revenue receipts of the Stale js contributed by Central tr"ansfers
comprising the State's share in Union pool of taxes and duties and grants-in-
aid fronr the GOI during 20A7-08. During the current :/ear, around 98.6 per
cent of the incremental revenue receipts were contributed by Central transfers
relative to previous year. The expenditure pattern of the State reveals that
although the revenue expenditure as a percentage of total expenditure declined
from 85 per cent in 2003-04 to 67 per cent in tire current year, NPRE
continued to share the dominant ploportion (79 per cent) driring the current
year" Tlre IIPRE at Rs.i,8i2 crore in 2007-08 remained significantly higher
than the nonnatiorely assessed levei of Rs.1563 crore by TFC for the year as
well as the projections made by the State Government in its FCP andMTFPS
for 2007-08. Further, the salaries and wages, pensions, interest pal,ments
and subsidies continued to consume a major share of NPRE, which was
around 77 per cent during 2007-08. The continued prevalence of fiscal
deficit during the period 2003-08 except in the current year when the State
experienced frscal surplus, indicates increasing reliance of the State on
borowed funds, resrLlting in increasing fiscal liabilities of the State over this
period, rvhich stood at 79.4 per cent of the GSDP in 2007-08 and further
increases to 83 per cent afier incorpor:ating the contingent liabilities in the
fold of total liabiiities on Consolidated Funil of the State dr-rring the year.
This is high especially if compared with the norm of 31 per cent to be

7.2.1 The Fiscal P.esponsibitity and Budget Management Act
lhe State Government enacted the Fiscal Responsibility and i3udget
I,[anagernent (FRBIT{) Act. 2Ct05 in August 2005 and framed the FRBIv{ Rules
in December 2005, to ensure prudence in fiscal management anci fiscai
stability by progressive elimination of revenue deficit, reduction in fiscal
deficit, prudent debt management consistent r,r,ith fiscal sustainabilit;r,. greater
liscal transparency in fiscal operations of the Government and conduct of
fiscal polic,v in a medium term frarner,vork. To girre e1I"ect to the tlscal
management principles, the Act prescribed the fcrillu'r"i": fiscal targets for t re
f-iol,ernrrrent to strive for:
remain re'{ienue surplus b-v reducing revenue expencliture ancl bLrild up
further surplus:
bring clown the fiscal r*leficil ta 3 per cent o{ GSDP by 2008-09:
r Lirnit the amount of our:tending (iorrermnent guarantees i],s per
provisions of Manipur Ceiiing on State Gol,ernment Guarantee Act,
2004.
o Fcllorv recntil.ment and rvage polic-v so that the expenditure on salary'
dces not exceed 35 per cenr ai the exoess of,revenue expenditurre ovei'
interest and pension payments.
Iior reciuction ofl trevcr1ur1 deficit and fiscai del'rcit, as laid dor'rr in the Act, the
$tate Governrnent frarned the FRBM Rules, tvhich state that the State
Gorrerflment shail strive to rednce the fiscal deficit b5,' a. minimum of 1 7:rer
cent af the GSDP by the end of each financial year, beginning u,ith the
financiai ."-ear 2005-06 so as to achieve the target of reduction of fisc*l detlcit
to 3 per cent by 2008-09 provided that. in tlie event of shortfall in the
reduction of revenue anii fiscal deficit as envisaged, the target of reduciion ol
deficit in the succeeding year shall stand enhanced by the amount of shortfail
in the preceding year.

1.2.3 Fiscal Policy Statement(s)
As prescribed in the Act, the State Government was to ia,v in each year the
following statements of fiscal policy along with the budget before the
legislature:
o The Medium Term Fiscal Policy Statement (N4TF-PS) and'
o The Fiscal Policy Strategy Statement.
As per MTFPS, revenue surplus was projected at Rs.40'4.01 crore by IVIarch
2008 lower than the projection made in FCP, fiscal detjcit was to be restricted
to Rs.i06 crore, higtrrer than the projection made in FCP. and totai outstanding
liabilities were to be restricted to Rs.4,210.47 crore.
1.2.4 Mid-Year Revierv
As per the FRBM Act, the State Government is reqriired to revier,v its reccipts
and expenditure on a quarteriy basis. However, as reliable data could not be
received from the departments on time, the State Goverrunent decided to
conduct a detailed review afler the data is fuliy received. T'he State
Government is taking necessary steps for completion of computerization of
treasuries so that the revielv could be conducted on the basis of authentic and
correct data in a timelv manner.
1.2.5 Fiscal performance
In terms of an incentive scheme of l'FC, a rervard for fiscal pc'rf,ormance w'as
built into the debt-write off package under DCRF'2. According to the scheme,
the quanturn of i.r.nite oif of repayment of the GOI loans after consolidation
and re-sclredulernent wiil be linked to the absolute amount by n'hich revenue
deficit is reduced in each successive year during the ar.vard perir:d. in e;lTect, if
the revenue deficit is brought to zero, the entire repa,vment during the period
will be written off. For States, u,hich r,vere in revenue surplus, as per the base
year figure3 and continue to remain so in the subsequent years till the end of
award period, the installment of repayment due on the Central loans may be
written-off in each of the years from 2005-06 onwards so long as the revenue
surplus of the States does not go below the base year level in absolute terms.
As a result of improved fiscal perforrnance in terms of this criterion, Manipur
Government received a debt $'aiver of Rs,37.54 crore duritlg 2007-08.

d-vls achievements are given below:

1.5.1 Quality of Expenditure
The availability of better social and physical infrastructure in the State reflects
its quality of expenditure. Therefore, the ratio of capital expenditure to total
expenditure as well as to GSDP and proportion of revenue expenditure spent
on running the existing social and economic services efficiently and
effectively rnould determine the quality of expenditure. The higher the ratio of
these components to total expenditure and GSDP, the better is the quality of
expenditure. The table below gives these ratios for the period 2003-08'
The capital expenditure relative to the total expenditure as well as to the
GSDP has been steadily rising over the last five years. Capital expenditure
was mainly incurred on Transport (Rs.230 crore), Water Supply, Sanitation &
Housing (Rs.172 crore), Inigation & Flood Control (Rs.164 crore), Power
(Rs.153 crore) and Education, Sports, Art & Culture (Rs.120 crore). The share
of revenue expenditure in the total expenditure on the other hand has been
declining over the years indicating a shift towards capital expenditure. Within
the revenue expenditure, the share of salary component (Social and Economic
Services) has gradually declined from 33 per cent in 2003-04 to 28 per cent rn
2007-08 while the share of non-salary component (Social and Economic
Services) has correspondingly increased fuam 24 to 3l per cenl during this
period. Assuming that capital expenditure incurred is on creating physical and
social intiastructure and non-salary component of revenue expenditure is on
efficient running and maintenance of social and economic services, then trends
presented in the table would tend to indicate improvement in quality of
development expenditure in the State during the period.

a strong linkage with eradication of poverty and economic progress, it would

1.6.4 Management of cash balances
It is generally desirable that State's flow of resources should match its
expenditure obligations. However, to take care of any temporary mismatches
in the flow of resources and the expenditure obligations, a mechanism of
Ways and Means Advances from RBI has been put in place. The operative
lirnit for Normal Ways and Means Advances is reckoned on the three year
average of revenue receipts and the operative limit for Special Ways and
Means Advances is fixed by the RBI from time to time depending on the
holding of Government securities. The limit for Normal Ways and Means
Advances has been fixed at Rs.60 crore while Special Ways and Means
Advances has been fixed up to a maximum of Rs.4.29 ctare against the pledge
of GOi securities.
During 2007-08, the State Government had to resort to ways and means
advance of R.s.38.79 crore for seven days for which an interest of Rs. three
lakh had to be incurred. The details are depicted in the table below:

Status of Guarantees - Contingent Liatlilities
Guarantees are liabilities contingent on the Consolidated Fund of the State in
case of default by the borrower to whom the guarantee has been extended.
As per Section 3 of the Manipur Ceiling on State Government Guarantee Act,
2004 (Act), the total outstanding guarantees as on I April of any year shall not
exceed thrice the State's own tax revenue receipts of the second preceding
year. During the current year the outstanding amount of guarantees was
reduced to Rs.211 crore from last year figure of Rs.25l crore. The outstanding
amount was also kept within the limit of the Actibid.

crore). No guarantee fee has, however, been realized during these years. The
State Government had set up (February 2008) a guarantee redemption fund to
meet the contingent liabilities arising from such guarantees, as per the
recommendation of the TFC but had not transferred any amount in this Fund
during the current year.
1.7.2
1.7.3 Debt Sustainability
Debt sustainability is defined as the ability of the State to maintain a constant
debt-GSDP ratio over a period of time and also embodies the concern about
the ability of the State to service its debt. Sustainability of debt therefore also
refers to sufficiency of liquid assets to meet current or committed obligations
and the capacity to keep balance between costs of additional borrowings with
returns from such borrowings. It means that rise in fiscal deficit should match
the increase in capacity to service the debt. A prior condition for debt
sustainability is debt stabilization in terms of debt/GSDp ratio.
1.7.4 Debt Stabilization
A necessary condition for stability states that if the rate of growth of economy
exceeds the interest rate or cost of public borrowings, the debt-GDp ratio is
likely to be stable provided primary balances are either zero or positive or are
moderately negative. Given the rate spread (GSDP growth rate-interest rate)
and quantum spread (debt x rate spread), debt sustainability condition states
that if quantum spread together with primary deficit is zero, debt-GSDp ratio
would be constant or debt would stabilise eventually. on the other hand, ifprimary deficit together with quantum spread turns out to be negative,
debt-GSDP ratio would be rising and in case it is positive, debt-GsDp ratio
1'

expenditure consistentl.v declined fiom 85 per cent in 2003-04 to 67 per cent

expenditure to total expenditure. The ratio of revenue receipts to total
expenditure during the pericd 20t13-C)7 also declined indicating that
dependence on boruuu,ed lunds has rleclined during these years. This is also
reflected in the declining ratio of liscal iiabilities to re\rcnue receipts during
2003-07. Increasing proliortiorl of plan u:xlienditure iiri,.i *apital expenditure
in the total expenditure aiso indicates irnprovenr,ent in the quality of
expenditure.
'fhe sharp increase o1'revenue surplus of lts.768 crore had a positive irnpact in
reversing fiscai deficit and piimrry deficit to surplus. T'he Balance from
Current Revenues (B[iR) also ti"lrneri ttr positive figure riuring the current year.

The fiscal pnsition of the State vicrved in tenns of ttre key fiscal parameters -
revenue surplus, fisc:rl deilcit and primarl, deficit - indicated significant
improvement riuring 2007-tl8 r:ver tl:e previotis year. While revenue surplus
nearly tripled, both llscal ai:d prim.:1r}. cli:ircits turned into surplus rlurirrg the
current ,vear" The targets set by FltItM Art as r,veli as liy' TFC/IjCP/N{TFPS in
terms of deficir indicators vrere achi*rred eariier than the time iimit set fbr
them" 'fhe impr*r,efi1ent in fiscal posirion of the State should however be
qonsitiereci keeping in vierv the fact that signiiicant share (exceeding 90 per
cent) of reveilue receipts of the State is contributed by' Central transfers
comprising of States' share in tlnion pooi of taxes and duties and grants-in-aid
trom the GOI during the period 2003-08 and during the current year, around

increase in Central transfers relative to previous year. The expenditure pattern

2.9.2 Llnarljusted Abstract Contingency bills: As per Rule 308 of Central
'freasury Rules (CTR), Abstract Contingent (AC) bills must be tbllowed by
the submission of detailed countersigned contingent (DCC) bills to adjust the
alxount drawn on AC bill. The CTR further stipulates that while drawing an
AC bill the draw-ing officer must attach a ceftificate to each such bill to the
effect that DCC bills have been submitted to the controlling offlcer in respect
of AC biils drawn more than a month before the date of that bill and on no
account an z\C bill should be encashed without this certificate (Rule 309).
Fr.rriher it says that the controlling officer must submit the DCC bills to the
Accountant General lvithin one month from the date of receipt of the DCC
biils in his offrce (Note 4 under Rule 312).
Scrutiny of rhe records revealed thaI663 AC biils involving Rs.601.48 crore
drawn by 47 Depafiments during the period April 2003 to March 2008 were
outstanding to be adjusted through DCC bills. Major portion of this money
(Rs.351.19 crore) relates to construction works entrusted to entities such as
Iv{anipur Police Housing Corporation, Manipur Development Soeiet,v,
Manipur Tribal Development Corpnration etc.

3.1.7 Planning
As per guidelines, fhe State Government should prepare a Perspective Plan
after a thorough analysis of gaps in inftastructure under various sectors. The
projects for consideration under NLCPR should be picked up strictly fiom the
perspective plan and according to the priority assigned therein. However, the
Planning Department did not prepare a gap analysis or Perspective Plan.
Consequently, the priority accorded to various projects in the Annual Plans
lacked justification. During exit conf,erence (October 2008) the Department
accepted the inadequacies in the planning process and assured that necessary
amends would be made in this regard.

Appendix 1.1
(Reference: Paragraphs 1.1 and 1.2)
Part A
Structure and Form of Governrnent Accounts
Structure of Government Accounts: The accounts of the State Got'ernment are kept
tn three parts (i) Consoiidated Fund (ii) Contingency Fund and (iii) Public Account.
Part tr: Consolidated Furld
A11 revenues received by the State Government, all loans raised by issue oi. trea.sury
bilis, internai ancl external loans and all mor,eys received by tlie Governrient in
repayrnent of loans shail ftlrm one consolidated fund titled 'The Consolidated Fund o1'
State' established under Articie 26b (\) of the Constitution of India.
Part II: Contingency Fund
Contingency Funcl of State esrablished r,inder Articie 267 (?) of the Constitution is rn
the nature of an rmprest placeri ar the disposal of the Governor to enable htm to make
advances to rneet urgellt unfbreseen expendihre. pending authorisation by the
Legisiature. Approval of the Legislature for surch expenditure and for withdrarval of
an equivalent amount iion"r the Consolidated Irund ts subsequentl-v obtaineri"
rvhereupon the acivatr.ces fi"orn tlie Contingency F'und are recouped to the Fund"
Part II{: Fublic Account
Receiprs and disbursements in respect of certain transactions such as smail savings,
provident funds, reserve funds, deposits, suspense. remittance etc. which do not form
part of the Consoliciated Fr:n<l, are kept in the Public Account set up under Article 266
(2) of the Constiiution anC are not subject to vote by the State f,egislature.
